<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Lineup composition affects identifications.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Eyewitness testimony can be unreliable.\\\\\\" (Overshoots by dropping the concession.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 18, \\"prompt\\": \\"Nutrition guidance once ranked dietary fats by their chemistry, condemning saturated varieties wholesale and promoting substitutes with more favorable laboratory profiles. Long-term population studies complicated the ranking by showing that health outcomes track what replaces a discarded fat: swapping it for refined starches yields no benefit, while swapping it for certain plant oils does. The chemistry was not wrong, and some substitutions really are improvements. The revision is in the unit of advice. Foods and their replacements, not molecules in isolation, have become the thing guidance is written about, because a nutrient\'\'s effect proved inseparable from what fills its place at the table.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Nutrition advice shifted from ranking fat molecules to evaluating whole substitutions, because outcomes depend on what replaces a discarded fat, even though the underlying chemistry and some swaps remain valid.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old unit: molecule-level rankings · (2) complication: outcomes track the replacement, not the removal · (3) retained concession: chemistry holds and some substitutions genuinely help; the change is the unit of advice. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Replacing fat with refined starch does not help.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Nutrition science keeps changing its mind.\\\\\\" (The cynical overshoot; the paragraph says the opposite.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 19, \\"prompt\\": \\"Flood control on large rivers was for a century an engineering contest: raise the levees, straighten the channel, move the water through faster. Repeated levee failures shifted the field toward a different accounting, in which the cheapest place to put floodwater is often a farm field purchased for occasional inundation rather than a wall built to exclude the water entirely. Engineered defenses still protect cities, and no one proposes removing them. The change is in what counts as infrastructure: acquired floodplain, wetland easements, and setback levees now appear in project budgets alongside concrete, because the failures showed that exclusion alone was purchasing risk at compound interest.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"After repeated levee failures, flood control expanded from pure exclusion engineering to budgeting land that can safely flood as infrastructure, while hard defenses still protect cities.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old contest: higher levees, faster channels · (2) shift: purchased floodplain and setbacks count as infrastructure · (3) retained concession: engineered defenses remain; exclusion alone compounded risk. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Some farm fields are bought to hold floodwater.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Levees sometimes fail.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 20, \\"prompt\\": \\"Histories of literacy long measured it by signatures: count who could sign a parish register, and map reading across a society. Scholars now emphasize that signing was taught after reading and separately from it, so signature counts systematically undercount readers, capturing mostly those schooled long enough to write, and excluding the many taught to read scripture at home who never held a pen. Signature evidence has not been discarded; nothing else survives at comparable scale, and its trends over time remain informative. What changed is the claim it supports: a floor under past literacy rather than an estimate of it.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Because writing was taught after reading, signature counts undercount past readers, so historians now treat them as a floor under literacy rather than an estimate, while still relying on their trends.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old measure: signatures as literacy · (2) flaw: reading preceded writing, so readers are missed · (3) retained concession: the evidence survives at unmatched scale and still shows trends; its claim is downgraded to a floor. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Many people could read but not sign.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Measuring historical literacy is hard.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 21, \\"prompt\\": \\"Early quantitative studies of scientific careers measured productivity by publication counts and found that a small fraction of researchers produced most of the papers, a result read for decades as evidence of rare individual talent. Later work complicated both the measure and the reading. Counting papers weights fields unequally, since publication rates are set by disciplinary custom rather than effort, and the concentration itself proved to be manufactured partly by institutions: early access to equipment, students, and funding compounds, so that small initial differences in opportunity grow into large final differences in output. Talent has not been dismissed as a factor. The revision is subtler: concentration of output is now treated as a joint product of ability and cumulative institutional advantage, and the measure that revealed it is no longer trusted to compare across fields.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"The finding that a few scientists produce most papers is now explained as talent compounded by accumulating institutional advantage rather than rare ability alone, and raw publication counts are no longer trusted across fields.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big …3409 tokens truncated…o automated path knows a session happened. Only a time-based rule or a manual one is implementable.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Assignment mechanics (Tier 3)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`classroom_items` is the universal student feed.** One row per thing the student sees. `type` covers drill, homework, activity, bootcamp_module, note, session_recap, announcement, weekly_plan_task and more. `visible_to_student` + `unlock_at` + `status` control exposure, enforced in RLS.\\r\\n- **Per-student payload tables:** `homework_assignments`, `activity_assignments`, `ta_assignments`, `guided_drill_assignments`. Each is a snapshot keyed by `student_id`. `homework_sets` is an admin-only template that never reaches a student.\\r\\n- **A precedent for Tier 3 already exists, twice.** `BootcampGate` (`src/components/hub/BootcampGate.tsx`) checks for an assigned `classroom_items` row and shows \\"Not assigned yet\\" otherwise. The `/bootcamp/*` routes are wrapped in BootcampGate **alone, with no ProtectedRoute and no flag**. `Bootcamps.tsx` does the same in list form. Both bypass entirely for admins.\\r\\n- **Route guards are per category, never per item.** `/classroom/:assignmentId`, `/classroom/item/:itemId` and `/classroom/ta/:assignmentId` all use `has_classroom_access` (note: the TA route does *not* use `has_ta_access`). `/activities/:assignmentId` uses `has_activity_access`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The Tier 3 trap — a timed section needs two flags.** Joshua assigns a timed section as a `homework_set` whose qids share one PT and section. `/section/:assignmentId` is guarded by `has_classroom_access`, but pressing GO launches into `/drill`, which is guarded by `has_drill_access` (`DrillLaunchGate`). So a Tier 3 student with drill off can open the directions screen and then hit a dead GO button. Assignment-only access cannot work while the runners stay flag-gated.\\r\\n\\r\\n**A full practice test is not assignable at all.** `practice_test_runs` is self-started (`user_id` defaults to `auth.uid()`); the Testing Centre has no assignment lookup. `/testing` is guarded by `has_drill_access`. So a whole practice test is bought by a flag, not granted per item. This is the main gap for an assignment-only tier.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Summary / key decisions\\r\\n\\r\\nAll confirmed by Joshua unless marked otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The model\\r\\n\\r\\n- A **tier is a template**. Selecting it applies a set of switch positions. The switches stay the thing the app checks, so nothing downstream changes.\\r\\n- **One tier per student.** No stacking.\\r\\n- **12 switches**, not 13: the two Wrong answer switches merge into one.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The tiers\\r\\n\\r\\n| Tier | Meaning | Applied how |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **Tier 1** | Every feature on | Manually by Joshua only. Indefinite, never expires. |\\r\\n| **Tier 2** | Joshua picks each feature individually | Manually. Instant effect. The toggles he sets persist as the student\'s state. What is *not* kept is a snapshot: leaving Tier 2 and returning does not restore an earlier set. |\\r\\n| **Tier 3** | Only what Joshua assigns, plus the floor | Manually, or by pressing X in the signup queue. |\\r\\n| **(no tier)** | Undecided. Same access as Tier 3, but sits in the queue | The state every new signup starts in. |\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The floor** (Tier 3 and no-tier alike): Classroom home, Messages, Schedule. Nothing else.\\r\\n- **Tier 1 is all-on by definition.** Turning any switch off relabels the student Tier 2. The relabel changes the label only, never access.\\r\\n- **Assigned work authorises its own runner**, following the existing BootcampGate pattern. An assigned item opens regardless of switch state. This is what makes Tier 3 mean what it says.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The governing rule\\r\\n\\r\\n**\\"The access should never change by itself.\\"** Stated twice by Joshua. Consequences:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Payment no longer grants access. The existing `grantStudentAccess` automation is removed and replaced by a notification.\\r\\n- No expiry, no automatic downgrade, no automatic upgrade.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The signup queue\\r\\n\\r\\n- Lives on **both** the admin homepage (Today) and the **Students** tab.\\r\\n- Contains brand-new signups **and** everyone with no tier.\\r\\n- **Checkmark** assigns a tier (1, 2 or 3) inline.\\r\\n- **X** puts them on Tier 3 with nothing assigned, which is functionally no access. This empties the queue properly and leaves them on the student list.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Migration day\\r\\n\\r\\n- Everyone keeps their current access, with one deliberate exception.\\r\\n- 19 with everything on -> **Tier 1**.\\r\\n- 6 partial -> **Tier 2** with current toggles kept.\\r\\n- 14 classroom-only -> **Tier 3**, and they **regain Messages and Schedule**. This is the one intentional widening: the floor exists so a locked-out student can ask why.\\r\\n- Rewiring the mislabelled switches must not change anyone\'s effective access.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Archive\\r\\n\\r\\n- Archiving remembers the tier and keeps access off. Unarchiving shows the previous tier; Joshua still chooses what to reopen.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Round 7 answers\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Q26 - Per-practice-test assignment:** **Recommendation rejected. Build it.** \\"there\'s only specific practice tests I do assign... I want them to be specific to whatever I assign specifically only that specific thing they can get. We don\'t have to assign them for the whole feature forever; just specifically that one individual practice test section or drill or whatever it is.\\"\\r\\n- **The governing principle this establishes:** for Tier 3, access is granted **per item, never per feature**. Assigning one practice test must not hand over the Testing Centre. Assigning one drill must not hand over the question bank. This applies to every assignable kind, with no exceptions.\\r\\n- **Q27 - Which runners accept an assignment:** **Answered by implication** - \\"that one individual practice test section or drill or whatever it is\\" covers every kind. All runners learn to accept an assignment: Drill (timed sections, homework, guided drills), Activities, TA worksheets, and now the Testing Centre.\\r\\n- **Scope consequence:** full practice tests need a new per-student assignment path. `practice_test_runs` is currently self-started (`user_id` defaults to `auth.uid()`) with no assignment table, so this is genuinely new work rather than a rewire.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Round 8 answers (final)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Q28 - What counts as one practice test:** **A practice test means the full sitting.** \\"If I say \'practice test,\' it should be a practice test. If I want to assign a section, then build a side section.\\" So there are two distinct assignable kinds, not one: a **Practice test** (the full Testing Centre sitting) and a **Section** (one timed 35-minute section). Do not collapse them. Sequencing between the two was not specified and is an implementation decision for the plan.\\r\\n- **Q29 - Do students see their tier:** **No.** Tier is operator language only.\\r\\n- **Q30 - History of tier changes:** **Not wanted.** Recommendation rejected; no audit trail.\\r\\n- **Q31 - Do tiers touch anything else:** **No.** Tiers control feature access only. Nothing to do with booking rates, session length or pricing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Open flags\\r\\n\\r\\nNone. Every branch answered.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Status\\r\\n\\r\\n**Complete.** Interview finished 2026-09-22. Frontier empty. Awaiting Joshua\'s confirmation that the write-up reads right before any code is written.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Resume point\\r\\n\\r\\nNext step is a written implementation plan built from the Summary above, for Joshua\'s approval. Three items from earlier in the same session remain outstanding and interact with this work:\\r\\n1. The PR #130 migration is not applied to the live database.\\r\\n2. The frontend is not published to lsatprep.study.\\r\\n3. The 14 revoked students are still classroom-only. Their fix is now folded into this plan\'s migration (they become Tier 3 and regain Messages and Schedule), so it should not be done separately.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Round 6 answers\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Q23 - What X does:** X puts them on Tier 3. Confirmed.\\r\\n- **Q24 - The 14 revoked students:** Tier 3, regaining Messages and Schedule. Confirmed.\\r\\n- **Q25 - Archive and tiers:** Remember the tier, keep access off. Confirmed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Q&A log\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 1\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q1 - What a tier actually is (tier computes the flags vs replaces them)\\r\\n- Asked: Should a tier be a label that computes the 13 flags, or a new column that replaces them?\\r\\n- Recommended: Tier computes the flags. Add `access_tier`, keep the flags as the enforcement layer.\\r\\n- Captured: **Not answered directly.** Joshua answered in UI terms instead: \\"those should be on the students page. When I clicked tier two, it should have given me tier two. Let\'s have a see all the options.\\"\\r\\n- Tentative: His phrasing implies the tier is a control on the Students page, and that picking Tier 2 reveals the full feature list to toggle. That is consistent with the recommendation but is not an explicit answer on the data model.\\r\\n- Flags: Re-ask in Round 2 -> Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q2 - One tier at a time, or stacking\\r\\n- Asked: Can a student hold more than one tier at once?\\r\\n- Recommended: Exactly one tier at a time.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"students should not be able to have more than one tier.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q3 - Where a new signup lands\\r\\n- Asked: What tier does a brand-new account get at first sign-in?\\r\\n- Recommended: Tier 3 (assigned-only), with a floor of classroom home, messages, schedule.\\r\\n- Captured: **Answered, and narrower than recommended.** \\"every time my student enrolls, the default should be no access, just the homepage, and to be able to schedule.\\" He named **two** items: homepage and schedule. He did not name messages.\\r\\n- Flags: Conflicts with his own Q5 answer (see below) -> Joshua to resolve in Round 2.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q4 - When Tier 1 access ends\\r\\n- Asked: Does Tier 1 full access ever expire on its own?\\r\\n- Recommended: Never auto-expires; downgrade by hand.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"tier one should be ... indefinitely.\\"\\r\\n- Supporting fact: the research above shows no automated path knows a session was attended, so an attendance-based expiry was not buildable anyway.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q5 - What a Tier 3 student sees with nothing assigned\\r\\n- Asked: What stays open for an assigned-only student before anything is assigned?\\r\\n- Recommended: A floor of three - classroom home, messages, schedule.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed as recommended.** \\"when I assign something, what you recommended is the right way to go.\\"\\r\\n- Flags: The recommendation he accepted includes **messages**, which his Q3 answer omitted. Unresolved conflict, do not silently pick one.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 2\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q6 - Tier sets the switches, or replaces them (re-ask of Q1)\\r\\n- Recommended: The tier sets the switches; keep the switches as the enforcement layer.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"there should be a toggle for each tier, and then it executes it. This is like templates. Yes.\\"\\r\\n- Decision: A tier is a template. Selecting it applies a set of switch positions. The 13 switches remain what the app checks.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q7 - Does the default include Messages\\r\\n- Recommended: Yes, include Messages, or a locked student has no way to ask why.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"it should include messages.\\" Conflict from Round 1 resolved: the floor is **three** items - Classroom home, Messages, Schedule.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q8 - What did he click\\r\\n- Captured: **\\"that was an accident.\\"** No competing tier system is being built in Lovable. We are building fresh.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q9 - What triggers Tier 1\\r\\n- Recommended: Keep the existing automatic payment trigger and raise it to all 13 switches.\\r\\n- Captured: **Rejected the automatic part.** \\"it should only be where I toggle tier one by myself.\\" Tier 1 is applied manually by Joshua, not automatically by a payment or a booking.\\r\\n- Flags: This contradicts live behaviour. `grantStudentAccess.ts` already fires automatically on every Stripe payment and turns on 9 switches. Must decide whether that automation is removed -> Round 3.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q10 - How an assigned item runs when its switch is off\\r\\n- Recommended: (a) the BootcampGate pattern - an assigned item authorises its own runner.\\r\\n- Captured: **Not answered.** Re-ask in Round 3 -> Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q11 - What happens to the existing 41 people\\r\\n- Recommended: Map each person to the tier matching their current switches so nobody\'s access changes on the day.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"for everybody right now, their access should stay the same.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 3\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q12 - How an assigned item runs when its switch is off\\r\\n- Recommended: (a) the BootcampGate pattern - an assigned item authorises its own runner.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"it should basically authorize its own runner.\\" Assigned work opens regardless of switch state. This is the core mechanism of Tier 3.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q13 - The automatic payment grant\\r\\n- Recommended: Replace the automatic grant with a notification.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed, and extended into a new requirement.** \\"No need for the tiers to be triggered by the actual payment.\\" Plus the governing principle, stated twice: **\\"the access should never change by itself.\\"**\\r\\n- New requirement - a signup queue on the admin dashboard: when somebody signs up they appear on Joshua\'s admin homepage with an inline control (checkmark / X / slash-delete). The checkmark assigns a tier (1, 2 or 3) straight from the dashboard. The queue stays on the dashboard page.\\r\\n- Also: \\"Every time I change the toggle, it should automatically reflect that.\\" Already satisfied - `profiles` is in the realtime publication, so a switch change reaches an open tab immediately.\\r\\n- Existing machinery to build on: `notify_admins_on_signup` trigger fires on `profiles` INSERT; `notifications` rows carry `type=\'signup\'`; `ClassroomLayout` already counts unread signups.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q14 - Tier label drift when one switch is changed\\r\\n- Recommended: The student silently becomes Tier 2, because the template no longer describes them.\\r\\n- Captured: **Not answered.** Re-ask -> Joshua.\\r\\n- Note: his principle \\"access should never change by itself\\" concerns access, not the label. The recommendation changes only the label, never access, so the two do not conflict.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q15 - Does Tier 2 remember previous toggles\\r\\n- Recommended: No memory.\\r\\n- Captured: **Not answered (second time).** Re-ask -> Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q16 - The mislabelled switches\\r\\n- Recommended: Rewire to match the labels, keeping everyone\'s effective access identical on the day.\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"Please rewire them to match their labels, using simple and concise language so I can easily understand what they refer to.\\" Labels must be plain English.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 4\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q17 - Tier label drift when one switch is changed (third ask)\\r\\n- Recommended: They become Tier 2, because the template no longer describes them.\\r\\n- Captured: Joshua answered **\\"Tier 1 should automatically enable all of the features.\\"** That defines Tier 1 as all-switches-on rather than addressing the drift case directly.\\r\\n- **Inferred, flagged for correction:** if Tier 1 means every feature on, then a student with one feature off is by definition not Tier 1, so the label becomes Tier 2. Recorded as the working decision. Joshua to correct if he meant something else.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q18 - Does Tier 2 remember previous toggles\\r\\n- Recommended: No memory.\\r\\n- Captured: \\"it doesn\'t have to remember.\\"\\r\\n- **Corrected 2026-09-22, same session.** \\"No memory\\" was the interviewer\'s wording and was too broad. Joshua\'s correction: \\"it should save what I assign or which feature that I toggle on but that\'s about it.\\"\\r\\n- **Precise decision:** the toggles Joshua sets on a Tier 2 student **are saved** - they are the student\'s switch state and persist normally. What is not kept is a **snapshot of a previous Tier 2 configuration**: moving Tier 2 -> Tier 1 -> Tier 2 does not restore the earlier set, because Tier 1 turned everything on and that is now the truth. No history, no stored configurations.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q19-Q22 - Signup queue location, the X action, queue contents, plain-English labels\\r\\n- Captured: **Not answered.** Re-asked in Round 5 -> Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Round 5\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q17 - Tier label drift (confirmed directly, superseding the Round 4 inference)\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed.** \\"I can\'t be able to turn off the drill if I have Tier One; they should become Tier Two.\\" The Round 4 inference was correct. Tier 1 is all-on by definition; any deviation relabels the student as Tier 2. Access is unchanged by the relabel.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q19 - Where the signup queue lives\\r\\n- Recommended: Today only.\\r\\n- Captured: **Answered, broader than recommended.** \\"It should just be on my homepage for Q19. And actually, I should see my student\'s tab as well.\\" The queue appears on **both** the admin homepage (Today) and the **Students** tab.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q20 - What the X does\\r\\n- Recommended: Archive them.\\r\\n- Captured: **Rejected archive.** \\"[X] should just not give them any access, leaving them in a queue or essentially like they\'ll be student lists but just not given any access.\\" So X means: no access, and they stay on the student list. Not archived, not deleted.\\r\\n- Flags: **Conflicts with the Q21 answer.** If X leaves them with no tier, and the queue is keyed on \\"no tier\\", they never leave the queue and X records nothing durable. Resolution needed -> Round 6 (Q23).\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q21 - What the queue contains\\r\\n- Captured: **Both.** \\"Brand new signups for question 21 and also everyone who has no tier.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Q22 - The plain-English labels\\r\\n- Captured: **Confirmed as written.** 12 labels after the two Wrong answer switches merge:\\r\\n  - Practice: Drill · Practice tests · Activities · Bootcamps\\r\\n  - Review: Wrong answers · Flagged questions · Progress and history · Export\\r\\n  - Classroom: Classroom · Messages · Schedule and booking · TA worksheets\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference: the 13 switches (supplied to Joshua, not a question)\\r\\n\\r\\n| Group | Label | Column |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Practice | Drill | `has_drill_access` |\\r\\n| Practice | Practice tests | `has_practice_access` |\\r\\n| Practice | Activities | `has_activity_access` |\\r\\n| Practice | Bootcamps | `has_bootcamp_access` |\\r\\n| Review | Wrong answers | `has_journal_access` |\\r\\n| Review | Wrong answer journal | `has_waj_access` |\\r\\n| Review | Flagged | `has_flagged_access` |\\r\\n| Review | Analytics and History | `has_analytics_access` |\\r\\n| Review | Export | `has_export_access` |\\r\\n| Classroom | Classroom | `has_classroom_access` |\\r\\n| Classroom | Messages | `has_chat_access` |\\r\\n| Classroom | Schedule | `has_schedule_access` |\\r\\n| Classroom | TA assignments | `has_ta_access` |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mislabelled switches - must be fixed as part of Tier 2, because Tier 2 exposes them all:**\\r\\n- `has_practice_access` \\"Practice tests\\" does **not** gate the Testing Centre; `/testing` uses `has_drill_access`. and Activity Controls: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes\\r\\nDate: 2026-09-12 · Goal: Define the teacher-facing controls for student access, activity visibility, and duration-based access in LSAT U.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Summary / key decisions\\r\\n\\r\\n- A teacher should be able to open a student and see the last time the student was active.\\r\\n- A teacher should be able to inspect the student\'s question and drill activity, with the exact scope and detail still to be defined.\\r\\n- A teacher should be able to toggle a student\'s platform access off from the teacher workspace.\\r\\n- Turning access off is an immediate, reversible suspension. The student\'s account, history, assignments, and messages remain intact.\\r\\n- Turning access off must not send the student an email.\\r\\n- A suspended student may reach an access-inactive notice, but every product area is locked: drills, questions, assignments, notes, messages, and normal sidebar navigation.\\r\\n- The student list or communication area should provide an easy numeric control for setting an access duration in days, with the exact behavior still to be defined.\\r\\n- The behavior when clicking a teacher\'s name in the teacher workspace is reported as needing a fix; the intended destination still needs clarification.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Q&A log\\r\\n\\r\\n### Q0 — Initial request\\r\\n- Asked: User initiated product discovery and invited a `/grill-me` interview.\\r\\n- Captured: Add student last-active visibility, comprehensive student activity history, a reliable teacher-workspace student detail interaction, an access on/off toggle, and a quick numeric days control for access duration.\\r\\n- Flags: Exact meanings, permissions, data detail, and expiration behavior remain to be resolved with Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Q1 — Meaning of turning access off\\r\\n- Asked: Should access-off immediately block all LSAT U content while preserving the student\'s account, history, assignments, and messages?\\r\\n- Captured: Yes. The student loses access immediately, but the account and all associated history, assignments, and messages remain intact. The action is reversible rather than deletion. It should not email the student, unlike the current behavior.\\r\\n- Flags: Define the exact suspended-student screen and any content or actions that remain available.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Q2 — Suspended-student experience\\r\\n- Asked: Should a suspended student retain messaging, or see only an inactive-access screen?\\r\\n- Captured: Lock everything. The state should resemble having no platform access before onboarding. The student gets no drills, questions, assignments, notes, messages, or other product areas. They should quickly understand that access is inactive. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"992aa6","wall_time_seconds":0.186778,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1334,"output":"import { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\nimport { tierFlags, type AccessTier, type ManagedUser } from \'@/lib/adminUsers\';\\n\\n/**\\n * Tier reads and writes that work whether or not the new admin-manage-users\\n * function is deployed. The frontend can ship ahead of the function (edge\\n * functions deploy through Lovable, separately from a publish), and an older\\n * function neither returns access_tier nor knows set_tier or the practice-test\\n * actions. Admins can read and write profiles directly under RLS, so each path\\n * falls back to the table when the function cannot do the job.\\n *\\n * Without the read fallback the People page would show every student as\\n * \\"No tier\\", and the signup queue would offer to put all of them on Tier 3.\\n */\\n\\n/** Fill access_tier from profiles when the list came from an older function. */\\nexport async function withAccessTiers(users: ManagedUser[]): Promise<ManagedUser[]> {\\n  if (users.length === 0 || users.some((u) => \'access_tier\' in u)) return users;\\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\\n    .from(\'profiles\')\\n    .select(\'class_id, access_tier\' as never);\\n  if (error) throw error;\\n  const tiers = new Map(\\n    ((data ?? []) as unknown as Array<{ class_id: string; access_tier: AccessTier | null }>).map((row) => [\\n      row.class_id,\\n      row.access_tier,\\n    ]),\\n  );\\n  return users.map((u) => ({ ...u, access_tier: tiers.get(u.class_id) ?? null }));\\n}\\n\\n/** The whole list, tiers included, from either function version. */\\nexport async function listManagedUsers(): Promise<ManagedUser[]> {\\n  const res = await supabase.functions.invoke(\'admin-manage-users\', { method: \'GET\' });\\n  if (res.error) throw res.error;\\n  return withAccessTiers(((res.data as ManagedUser[]) ?? []) as ManagedUser[]);\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Apply a tier: Tier 1 and Tier 3 rewrite the switches, Tier 2 keeps them and\\n * changes the label. Tries the function first; on an older function, writes\\n * the same single update to profiles. Throws unless the saved row shows the tier.\\n */\\nexport async function setStudentTier(classId: string, tier: AccessTier): Promise<void> {\\n  const res = await supabase.functions.invoke(\'admin-manage-users\', {\\n    method: \'POST\',\\n    body: { action: \'set_tier\', class_id: classId, tier },\\n  });\\n  const written = (res.data as { profile?: { access_tier?: string } } | null)?.profile;\\n  if (!res.error && written?.access_tier === tier) return;\\n\\n  const updates: Record<string, unknown> = { access_tier: tier, ...(tierFlags(tier) ?? {}) };\\n  if (tier !== \'tier_2\') updates.has_waj_access = false;\\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\\n    .from(\'profiles\')\\n    .update(updates as never)\\n    .eq(\'class_id\', classId)\\n    .select(\'class_id, access_tier\' as never);\\n  if (error) throw error;\\n  const row = (data ?? [])[0] as unknown as { access_tier?: string } | undefined;\\n  if (row?.access_tier !== tier) throw new Error(\'Tier was not saved\');\\n}\\n\\n/** Assign or unassign one PrepTest sitting, from either function version. */\\nexport async function setPracticeTestAssignment(\\n  classId: string,\\n  pt: number,\\n  assign: boolean,\\n  adminId: string | undefined,\\n): Promise<void> {\\n  const res = await supabase.functions.invoke(\'admin-manage-users\', {\\n    method: \'POST\',\\n    body: { action: assign ? \'assign_practice_test\' : \'unassign_practice_test\', class_id: classId, pt },\\n  });\\n  if (!res.error && (res.data as { success?: boolean } | null)?.success) return;\\n\\n  const sourceId = `practice_test:${pt}`;\\n  if (assign) {\\n    const { error } = await supabase.from(\'practice_test_assignments\' as never).upsert(\\n      {\\n        student_id: classId,\\n        pt,\\n        assigned_by: adminId ?? null,\\n        assigned_at: new Date().toISOString(),\\n        status: \'assigned\',\\n      } as never,\\n      { onConflict: \'student_id,pt\' },\\n    );\\n    if (error) throw error;\\n    const { data: existing } = await supabase\\n      .from(\'classroom_items\')\\n      .select(\'id\')\\n      .eq(\'student_id\', classId)\\n      .eq(\'type\', \'practice_test\' as never)\\n      .eq(\'source_id\', sourceId)\\n      .maybeSingle();\\n    if (existing) {\\n      const { error: updateError } = await supabase\\n        .from(\'classroom_items\')\\n        .update({ status: \'assigned\', visible_to_student: true } as never)\\n        .eq(\'id\', (existing as { id: string }).id);\\n      if (updateError) throw updateError;\\n    } else {\\n      const { error: insertError } = await supabase.from(\'classroom_items\').insert({\\n        student_id: classId,\\n        created_by: adminId ?? null,\\n        type: \'practice_test\',\\n        title: `PrepTest ${pt}`,\\n        description: \'A full practice test: three scored sections, in order, with a break.\',\\n        source_table: \'practice_test_assignments\',\\n        source_id: sourceId,\\n        status: \'assigned\',\\n        visible_to_student: true,\\n        metadata_json: { pt, href: \'/testing\' },\\n      } as never);\\n      if (insertError) throw insertError;\\n    }\\n  } else {\\n    const { error } = await supabase\\n      .from(\'practice_test_assignments\' as never)\\n      .update({ status: \'archived\' } as never)\\n      .eq(\'student_id\', classId)\\n      .eq(\'pt\', pt);\\n    if (error) throw error;\\n    await supabase\\n      .from(\'classroom_items\')\\n      .update({ status: \'archived\' } as never)\\n      .eq(\'student_id\', classId)\\n      .eq(\'type\', \'practice_test\' as never)\\n      .eq(\'source_id\', sourceId);\\n  }\\n}\\n"}'}]
